## Previous resumable checkpoint — Preserve storage state when mount inventory I/O fails

- **Exact code/test checkpoint:** branch `main`, implementation commit `4c305894ea1de24bdf6157f633a54ace7effc257`, based on `717113c7904eb55a05dd253859645f6c22820a9b`. The code, host regression, source-contract workflow and build-path triggers are committed; exact-head Actions [run 42457727317](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42457727317) completed **SUCCESS** on `4c305894ea1de24bdf6157f633a54ace7effc257`.
- **Module / priority:** USB/storage/hotplug, P1 stability and data-integrity protection. This is the first bounded finding in the resumed storage rotation, not a claim that the storage subsystem is fully audited.
- **Finding:** `count_stor_mountpoint()` returned zero when `/proc/mounts` could not be opened, and ignored read/close failures. Callers used that zero as proof that no USB/MMC storage was mounted. This made the existing `mount_count < 0` uncertainty branch in `safe_remove_stor_device()` unreachable for inventory I/O failures; notably, NFS support could be unloaded on an incomplete inventory, while other storage-dependent restarts silently skipped service startup.
- **Implementation:** define a tri-state contract: non-negative counts mean a complete inventory (zero is a proven empty state); `-1` means open/read/close failure. Preserve and log the underlying errno where available, falling back to `EIO`. FTP, Samba, DLNA, Firefly, Transmission, Aria and deferred storage-app starts now refuse to start on unknown inventory. NFS restart leaves support loaded and returns without unloading when inventory is unknown. Safe removal can now reach its existing uncertainty gate and unload NFS only after a complete zero-count inventory and successful cleanup.
- **Files:** `trunk/user/rc/src/services_stor.c`, `rc.h`, new `storage_mount_inventory_policy.h`, new `tests/storage_mount_inventory_policy.c`, and `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`.
- **Host regression:** GNU89 `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` compile and execution passed locally for the new tri-state policy test. Cases cover known-empty, known-nonempty, open/read/close failure mapping and the service-start predicate. Workflow source contracts verify `fopen` failure, `ferror`, `fclose`, both success/error returns, all storage-dependent restart callers, NFS no-unload-on-unknown behavior and the safe-remove uncertainty gate.
- **Exact-head CI:** GitHub Actions [run 42457727317](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42457727317) completed **SUCCESS** on exact code SHA `4c305894ea1de24bdf6157f633a54ace7effc257`. The new `Verify storage mount inventory distinguishes I/O failure from no mounts` step passed together with the existing storage, PPP, firewall, service-lifecycle and HTTPD regression steps.
- **Limits / residuals:** L1 source review and L2 host/Actions regression only. No MIPS/uClibc compile, firmware/TRX, router runtime, live USB/eject, or hardware test. On inventory failure, dependent services can remain stopped until a later retry/restart; this change deliberately does not treat unknown state as empty and does not add a retry scheduler. FAT/NTFS checker semantics, optware hook error handling, `create_mp_link()` inventory handling, mountpoint races, stale mounts and remaining swap/unmount paths are still open.
- **Next exact action:** audit `automount.sh` FAT and NTFS checker commands and exit-status contracts. Trace the actual `fsck.fat`, `chkntfs`, `ntfs-3g`, kernel-driver and RO fallback paths; determine whether nonzero/incomplete repair can reach an unsafe RW mount. Add focused host tests and workflow coverage for the evidenced case. Preserve broad whole-userland rotation and parallel security review.
- **Working state:** source/test/workflow changes are committed to `main` at `4c305894ea1de24bdf6157f633a54ace7effc257`; exact-head run 42457727317 is SUCCESS. No unfinished source patch or checkpoint branch is being carried. A documentation-only checkpoint commit follows this code commit.

## Previous resumable checkpoint — Fence stale WAN PPP callbacks across stop/restart

- **Exact tested code checkpoint:** branch `main`, source/workflow SHA `614aa7052d8ac3b9e92ebcbda1918f6ac7781888`. The implementation was introduced by `4e1fb8b0f89e610faf135308ec9511ccfd9795fd`; legacy-source encoding in CI was corrected by `086bde3055ae69bab728f2423fe94517afb7f2aa`; the service-stop source contract and bounded interrupted-lock retry were finalized in `614aa7052d8ac3b9e92ebcbda1918f6ac7781888`.
- **Remote task recovery:** the previous top handoff item explicitly named callback-generation fencing. No active remote PR or useful unfinished branch remained. The implementation traced the real producer rather than assuming an environment variable: pppd `ipcp.c::ipcp_script()` and `ipv6cp.c::ipv6cp_script()` both populate `argv[6]` from `ipparam`; the `rc.c` applet dispatcher passes the argument vector to the registered IPv4/IPv6 handlers. pppd's callback paths are under `/tmp/ppp/`.
- **Race:** the stop list names `ip-up`, `ip-down`, IPv6 callback workers, the L2TP transports and `pppd`. A callback may already be in flight or be launched during the name-based termination sweep. Name-based stopping alone does not prove that a callback will not subsequently mutate WAN NVRAM, interface, route or resolver state.
- **Implementation:** each WAN PPP launch advances a strictly parsed decimal session generation and appends its generated `ipparam` after the user-supplied `ppp_pppd` options. Free-form PPP options reject any user `ipparam` key so the token cannot be overridden. The root-owned private `/tmp/ppp/wan-session.state` file is created with `O_CREAT | O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`; existing files are opened without creation, checked as single-link regular files owned by the effective UID and forced to mode 0600. An empty generation is accepted only by the process that exclusively created the new file; a pre-existing empty/truncated state fails closed rather than reusing generation 1. A bounded `fcntl(F_SETLK)` lock serializes generation changes with complete callback execution.
- **Callback behavior:** IPv4 up/down and IPv6 up/down callbacks require `argc >= 7`, compare `argv[6]` against the active generation before side effects, hold the lock throughout their WAN state mutations, and make stale-session callbacks no-ops. `stop_wan_ppp()` and `stop_wan()` invalidate the generation before terminating services or clearing WAN state. A failed PPP launch invalidates the attempted token. Lock contention and interrupted lock attempts remain bounded and fail closed.
- **Files:** `trunk/user/rc/src/net_ppp.c`, `net_wan.c`, `rc.h`, `ppp_option_value_policy.h`, new `ppp_callback_generation_policy.h`, new `tests/ppp_callback_generation_policy.c`, `tests/ppp_option_value_policy.c`, and `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`.
- **Regression / exact-head CI:** new GNU89 `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` generation-policy test covers canonical token parsing, stale/current matches, monotonic advancement, overflow, malformed values, buffer limits and the rule that an empty state is valid only on exclusive first creation. Existing option escaping test now rejects user-supplied `ipparam`. Workflow source contracts verify the pppd IPv4/IPv6 argv producers, all four callback gates, stop-before-kill fencing and the extended failure return path. GitHub Actions [run 42457285021](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42457285021) completed **SUCCESS** on exact SHA `614aa7052d8ac3b9e92ebcbda1918f6ac7781888`; all 23 job steps passed, including PPP and bounded service/WAN stop regressions.
- **CI correction history:** run `42456900878` first exposed that legacy PPPD source is not UTF-8; workflow reads those two legacy source files as Latin-1. Run `42456945523` then exposed a stale stop-contract count (the new fail-closed generation guard adds one return path); the assertion was updated to require three early-exit paths and explicitly verify invalidation precedes PPP termination. Both are superseded by exact-head SUCCESS run 42457285021; that run also validates the exclusive-create/empty-state fail-closed follow-up.
- **Validation boundary / residuals:** L1 source/control-flow review and L2 GNU89 host tests plus exact-head Actions PASS. No MIPS/uClibc production compile, full firmware/TRX, live PPP/L2TP session, router runtime or physical hardware test. Target BusyBox `pids()/killall` behavior, runtime locking semantics on the firmware kernel/libc, reconnect/watchdog/backoff, `udhcpc` SIGUSR2 sequencing and other VPN PPP callback families remain outside this bounded fix. This closes the WAN PPP stale-callback race at source/host-CI level; it does not close the broader WAN/PPP audit.
- **Next task:** continue the whole-userland rotation into storage mount/swap/hotplug. Inspect `hotplug_stor.c`, `services_stor.c`, `libdisk/disk_initial.c`, and `automount.sh` / `storage_fsck_status.sh` / storage init scripts; find a concrete failed-unmount, stale-device, duplicated hotplug, partial-mount or resource-cleanup bug. Retain firewall, WAN reconnect and the global stability/security ledger.
- **Working state:** all code and test changes are committed to `main`; no local clone or uncommitted patch is being carried. Use `614aa7052d8ac3b9e92ebcbda1918f6ac7781888` and Actions run 42457285021 as the exact tested source checkpoint. Historical Adbyby/firewall checkpoints below remain history and are not the current next task.

## Previous resumable checkpoint — Roll back partial Adbyby multi-table firewall restores

- **Code checkpoint:** branch `main`; base `3e11346dface4f8b65ddf50cfcc2ff313ceedc2f`; implementation commit `c9de00d89e44068c53b687bb66fe5c269f3863d1` (`fix(firewall): roll back partial Adbyby table restores`). The documentation update containing this handoff is a follow-on commit; the code checkpoint SHA above is the exact tested source.
- **Defect closed:** `firewall_adbyby_restore_policy_apply()` replayed one multi-table `iptables-restore -n` file spanning IPv4 `filter` and `nat`. A later table failure could occur after an earlier table had committed, leaving a mixed extension/core ruleset without recovery.
- **Implementation:** take private full-table `iptables-save -t filter` and `iptables-save -t nat` snapshots before the extension mutation. If the multi-table restore fails, attempt full-table rollback in reverse order (NAT, then filter) without `--noflush`; report each failure. If either rollback fails, retain both snapshots, log their paths, disable IPv4 forwarding and abort the remaining extension-hook phase. Cleanup failures keep the paths tracked and are retried before another Adbyby restore. Missing `blockip` still skips the extension restore before any snapshot or mutation.
- **Files:** `trunk/user/rc/src/firewall_adbyby_restore_policy.h`, `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/firewall_adbyby_restore_policy.c`, `trunk/user/rc/src/firewall_ex.c`, `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`.
- **Regression coverage:** missing snapshot no-op; unavailable `blockip`; filter/NAT snapshot staging failure cleanup; failed multi-table restore rolls back NAT then filter; rollback failure preserves both recovery snapshots and blocks the next restore; cleanup failure retries before a later apply; successful restore/cleanup.
- **Verification:** exact-head workflow [42455968821](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42455968821) completed **SUCCESS** on `c9de00d89e44068c53b687bb66fe5c269f3863d1`. The workflow compiled and ran the policy regression under GNU89 with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`, plus the complete existing host/static regression job. This is L1/L2 source/host/CI evidence; it is not a production MIPS object build.
- **Not verified:** full MIPS/uClibc compile, firmware/TRX, router runtime, live iptables partial-commit behavior and physical hardware. Snapshot/apply/rollback is not serialized against unrelated external iptables writers; a concurrent writer could be overwritten by a rollback. This race remains a firewall residual, not a claim of global firewall transaction isolation.
- **Next task:** WAN/PPP callback-generation fencing. Start with `trunk/user/rc/src/net_wan.c` (`stop_wan_ppp()`, `stop_wan()`), `trunk/user/rc/src/net_ppp.c` (`ipup_main()`, `ipdown_main()`, and IPv6 callbacks), pppd's up/down script configuration and every related start/stop caller. The stop list currently names callback workers before `pppd`; determine whether pppd can spawn a callback after its name was checked/killed, and whether an already-running callback can mutate NVRAM, interfaces, routes or DNS after a new WAN session begins. Establish callback provenance and lifecycle before designing a generation fence; add a host-testable stale-session regression. Keep storage mount/swap/hotplug as the following rotation.
- **Working tree:** all implementation files are committed; no local clone is available in this execution environment. Do not treat the stale `fix/adbyby-ipset-restore-20261108` checkpoint as a source of unique changes (it compared 0 ahead of main at recovery).

## Previous resumable checkpoint — Block firewall reset while recovery state remains

> Historical checkpoint from before the Adbyby transactional rollback commit above. Its Adbyby finding and next-action lines describe the then-open issue; they are superseded by the latest checkpoint above.


- **Base code checkpoint:** `47d68c69d5a2e9b1ba5a89a55664bb50410f218c` (snapshot cleanup retry implementation; exact-head Actions run `42453352075` was SUCCESS on that SHA). The follow-up reset-gate helper, tests, production wiring and workflow contract landed in `2ba59280...`, `d85bd280...`, `3a329e93...` and `98f8e6d0...`; a final comment-only alignment is `cceb1c757b0354bc5541be903d4353244d2a15e3`. Current main may advance with the documentation updates below.
- **Follow-up finding:** the prior cleanup-retry code called `firewall_restore_transaction_cleanup_snapshots()` and then unconditionally reset the transaction. If the retry still failed, the retained path was lost again. More seriously, the caller deliberately skipped cleanup after `rollback_failed` but still reset the transaction, erasing the retained recovery-snapshot path records.
- **Remote checkpoint scan:** `fix/adbyby-ipset-restore-20261108` compares as 0 commits ahead / 107 behind `main`; no unique change remains to cherry-pick from that branch. Current `main` has no open PRs at this checkpoint. The separate `security/storage-ej-authorization-20261108` branch remains divergent (11 ahead / 147 behind) and its storage-EJ policy/test blobs were already verified byte-identical to main; do not restore that stale branch wholesale.
- **Fix:** added `firewall_restore_transaction_prepare_reset()`. It refuses reset when rollback failed; otherwise it retries all tracked snapshot removals and resets only after the tracked set is empty. `start_firewall_ex()` and standalone `firewall_apply_core_restore()` now refuse a new apply/rebuild with `EBUSY` when prior recovery state remains unresolved. Incomplete-rollback snapshots are logged and kept; cleanup failures keep their path list for another later retry.
- **Regression coverage added:** `test_prepare_reset_refuses_cleanup_failure_then_retries` verifies a repeated unlink failure leaves the path and transaction intact, then a later successful cleanup allows reset. `test_prepare_reset_preserves_failed_rollback_recovery_set` verifies no remover call occurs and all rollback snapshots remain available. Existing IPv6 filter/NAT cleanup retry and reverse rollback tests remain.
- **Validation:** a focused local GNU89 `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` harness for the new reset-gate state transitions passed. The repository's full `firewall_restore_transaction.c` host test and full GitHub Actions workflow have **not yet been verified for the new follow-up SHA**. The prior run `42453352075` only verifies `47d68c69...`, not this follow-up. The available GitHub workflow-run connector exposes PR-triggered runs but did not expose push-triggered runs for this mainline update, so no newer Actions result is claimed.
- **Limitations:** no MIPS/uClibc target compile, firmware/TRX, live iptables/ip6tables restore, router or hardware test.
- **New Adbyby audit finding (not fixed yet):** `firewall_adbyby_restore_policy_apply()` preflights the `blockip` ipset, then runs `iptables-restore -n` on a snapshot containing both filter and NAT tables. A nonzero restore status is only reported; no pre-restore table snapshot/rollback is performed. Since the file is multi-table and restore commits tables in sequence, an earlier table can remain changed when a later table fails. The current policy tests verify preflight/reporting but not partial-apply recovery. Treat this as the next P1 firewall task.
- **Next action:** make the Adbyby extension restore transactional across the exact tables it can mutate, test a filter-commit/NAT-failure case and rollback failure retention, then inventory other standalone IPv4/IPv6 writers. Rotate into storage/mount/swap, network-service lifecycle, authentication/upload/restore and boot/system management. WAN/PPP callback-generation fencing remains open; HTTPD and the overall repository audit remain PARTIAL. Do not narrow back to SkipDBv2-only work.

## Previous resumable checkpoint — Retry failed firewall restore snapshot cleanup

- **Main baseline:** `c49eab3250abe1fd0159ee933609fe862e62e1af`; this round continues the top handoff item after the direct ASP query-output fix.
- **Exact tested implementation/source head:** `47d68c69d5a2e9b1ba5a89a55664bb50410f218c`; later main commits are documentation-only verification/checkpoint updates.
- **Finding:** `firewall_restore_transaction_finish()` and successful rollback cleanup called a void remover and always cleared `snapshot_count`. If `unlink()` failed, a 0600 recovery snapshot remained in `/tmp` but the transaction forgot its path and treated cleanup as complete. Repeated firewall rebuilds could accumulate stale tmpfs files.
- **Implementation:** the remove callback now returns status; cleanup compacts and retains paths that failed to unlink, and tracks `cleanup_failed` separately from apply/rollback failure. Both full rebuild and standalone-table transaction resets retry pending cleanup. Re-entering abort retries cleanup without re-running rollback. A failed rollback still preserves all recovery snapshots.
- **Regression additions:** GNU89 transaction tests cover failed cleanup after a successful IPv6 filter apply, retry on finish, failed cleanup after a successful IPv6 NAT rollback, and retry on repeated abort while asserting rollback runs exactly once. Workflow contracts require these tests and the pre-reset retry wiring.
- **IPv6 scope review:** core table restore is family-aware for both `iptables` and `ip6tables`; startup order covers IPv6 mangle/NAT/filter and rollback is reverse application order across families. The IPv4-only `LWF_LIST` was not copied into IPv6 because the current LAN-WAN UI/schema exposes IPv4-only address inputs (15-character fields plus IPv4 validators), with no IPv6 rule data model. Treat this as a product-scope boundary, not a proven missing IPv6 setting.
- **Exact-head CI:** implementation/test/workflow commit `47d68c69d5a2e9b1ba5a89a55664bb50410f218c` is on `main`. GitHub Actions [run 42453352075](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42453352075) completed **SUCCESS** on that exact SHA; the full `HTTPD Authentication and Security Regression` job passed, including GNU89 `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` compile/run of `firewall_restore_transaction.c` and source-contract checks. No MIPS/uClibc target build, firmware/TRX, live firewall restore or router/hardware test is claimed.
- **Next action:** continue the broad firewall writer/ipset/extension audit, starting with Adbyby's saved restore file, `blockip` set creation/removal order and partial extension-restore failure handling (this restore remains outside the core transaction). Inventory other standalone `iptables`/`ip6tables` mutations; then rotate into storage/mount/swap or network/system-management while preserving WAN/PPP generation fencing and whole-userland scope.

## Previous resumable checkpoint — Escape reflected query values in direct ASP JavaScript

- **Code checkpoint on main:** exact validated source/workflow head is `de5418e7024db61842ec624014bed510e80a57fa`. The implementation was introduced in `b68ecd147bfd7543e802142aa7cc3e7c417bce59`, its policy test correction is `84c325f3563eeca96b326aa6a71f1317d8f17720`, and the final caller-inventory/CI contract is `de5418e7024db61842ec624014bed510e80a57fa`.
- **Remote checkpoint review:** stale branch `security/storage-ej-authorization-20261108` is at `5dfca9b8be8c865367243561d8cee6aef7db408a`, diverged from current main (11 commits ahead / 134 behind; merge base `ffdee3519d860d832d82fbd8a6aab46467a94163`), and has no open PR. Its core `httpd_storage_ej_policy.h` and `tests/httpd_storage_ej_policy.c` blobs are byte-identical to current main; its older CGI-method policy has since evolved on main. Do not cherry-pick this stale checkpoint or treat it as unfinished storage authorization work. No open PRs were present at inspection time.
- **Finding:** `web_ex.c::ej_get_parameter()` previously copied `websGetVar()` data directly to output with `websWrite(wp, "%s", value)`. `cgi.c::init_cgi()` URL-decodes percent-encoded query data, and six direct ASP templates interpolate the value inside single-quoted JavaScript literals. A crafted query value could therefore break out of the literal and inject script in an authenticated router UI response (reflected JavaScript/XSS sink).
- **Affected templates inventoried:** `aidisk.asp`, `index.asp`, `aidisk/getfolderarray.asp`, `aidisk/getsharearray.asp`, `aidisk/set_account_permission.asp`, and `Restarting.asp`. The CI contract checks this complete caller set and requires each existing template to retain the quoted JS-string context.
- **Fix:** added `httpd_js_output_policy.h` with a bounded, streaming JS-string-content encoder. It escapes apostrophes and backslashes, encodes `<` as `\\x3c` to prevent HTML `</script>` termination, encodes control characters, and escapes UTF-8 U+2028/U+2029 as JS Unicode escapes. `ej_get_parameter()` now emits escaped contents only because the six templates supply their own surrounding quotes. Existing `write_js_string()` reuses the same encoder, preserving and strengthening the existing string writer.
- **Classification and tests:** the explicit 88-callback direct-EJ table now classifies `get_parameter` as `HTTPD_DIRECT_EJ_ESCAPED_OUTPUT` rather than plain READ_ONLY. The revised totals are 62 READ_ONLY, 1 ESCAPED_OUTPUT, 3 BOUNDED_REFRESH, 2 SELF_GUARDED and 20 TRUSTED_MUTATION. Added `tests/httpd_js_output_policy.c` to test quotes, backslashes, script-closing tags, controls, U+2028/U+2029, and ordinary UTF-8. Direct-EJ class tests cover the new category.
- **CI:** exact-head GitHub Actions [run 42452014769](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42452014769) on `de5418e7024db61842ec624014bed510e80a57fa` completed **SUCCESS**. GNU89 `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` policy compile/run passed, as did the complete HTTPD/security regression workflow, including the six-template caller contract and the existing source-wiring checks.
- **CI correction history:** the first implementation run exposed a test inventory mistake (the escaped-output callback had accidentally remained in the READ_ONLY test list); it was corrected before the successful run. The next source-contract run exposed one additional nested Aidisk caller, `aidisk/set_account_permission.asp`; that caller was added to the exact inventory. Runs `42451918926` and `42451952876` failed on those respective test/contract issues; they are superseded by the exact-head SUCCESS above.
- **Validation boundary:** host regression and exact-head GitHub Actions PASS. No full MIPS/uClibc HTTPD target build, firmware/TRX image, browser exploit reproduction against a router, browser session/CSRF integration, or physical router/hardware test was performed. Treat this as a source-confirmed unsafe output sink that is now encoded, not as a claim of live-device exploit reproduction.
- **Remaining repository scope:** direct EJ authorization inventory is explicit and fail-closed for all 88 current handler registrations, and the reflected query-string JS sink is fixed. The overall HTTPD security audit remains PARTIAL; other callback output contexts and all non-HTTPD modules still need review. Next rotate to firewall IPv6 restore/cleanup parity (including rollback, forwarding fail-closed and rule-family consistency), then storage/mount/swap, network-service lifecycle, system administration/boot, authentication/upload/restore and other Padavan userland. Do not collapse the programme back to SkipDBv2-only work.

- **Audit coverage:** inspected the full web_ex.c::ej_handlers[] registry (88 registered callback names), the direct template callback bodies with security-sensitive effects, the direct ASP templates under trunk/user/www/n56u_ribbon_fixed (129 .asp files including nested aidisk/), ej.c::call_asp(), the storage EJ delegate, the script dispatch path, and the apply-request policy. The CI source contract now parses the complete registry and all embedded <% ... %> callback references, including nested templates.
- **Explicit classification:** all 88 registry entries are now assigned exactly one class in httpd_direct_ej_policy.h: 63 READ_ONLY; 3 BOUNDED_REFRESH (bandwidth, nvram_get_ddns, detect_internet); 2 SELF_GUARDED (nvram_dump, update_variables); 20 TRUSTED_MUTATION (the 9 existing direct privileged callbacks plus 11 storage mutators). Storage mutators retain the independent httpd_storage_ej_allowed() check as defense in depth.
- **Fail-closed behavior:** an unknown, removed, misspelled, or future EJ callback now returns UNKNOWN and is denied even when the caller passes a trusted session. CI requires the policy inventory to match the source registry exactly, with no duplicates, missing names, stale names, or unclassified entries. Adding a handler requires an explicit security class.
- **Self-guarded callback review:** nvram_dump was not treated as a blanket trusted-only operation because its script dispatch calls httpd_script_dispatch_allowed(name, get_login_safe()), the syscmd.sh execution branch separately checks get_login_safe() and clears stale command state otherwise, and dump_file() hides .key material when get_login_private_key_access() is false. update_variables checks httpd_apply_request_allowed() before applying service values or dispatching scripts. These existing internal checks are deliberately represented as SELF_GUARDED, not silently treated as simple read-only callbacks.
- **Bounded refresh classification:** bandwidth refreshes fixed rstats files / daemon state, detect_internet signals the fixed detection PID, and nvram_get_ddns clears a temporary DDNS result. They are separately named BOUNDED_REFRESH operations rather than being mixed with pure reads or trusted mutations.
- **Implementation:** httpd_direct_ej_allowed() now rejects UNKNOWN before dispatch and requires the existing trusted-session predicate for TRUSTED_MUTATION entries. The HTTPD security workflow gained exact source-registry/category parity checks and scans all direct ASP templates to ensure every embedded callback resolves to a registered handler. Workflow path filters include web_ex.c for both push and pull requests.
- **Changed paths in the final code commit:** trunk/user/httpd/src/httpd_direct_ej_policy.h; trunk/user/httpd/src/tests/httpd_direct_ej_policy.c; .github/workflows/httpd-security.yml.
- **Regression / exact-head CI:** GNU89 -Wall -Wextra -Werror policy compilation and execution passed, as did the complete HTTPD/security source-contract job on exact code HEAD 0b7e26f916e79550d2242bf3ee7437f41483f6d5: Actions run 42451502904 completed SUCCESS (https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42451502904). The corrected full ASP caller scanner also passed on 5d690dfe2b27ec08849caf46c74c3a358b9c109d: Actions run 42451377434 completed SUCCESS (https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42451377434). The earlier 8295ea2 workflow run passed its then-current tests, but its first version of the scanner regex was over-escaped and did not actually enumerate template tags; this was corrected in 5d690dfe and is not counted as scanner validation.
- **Validation boundary:** exact-head host CI PASS. No full target HTTPD build, MIPS/uClibc compile, firmware/TRX image, browser/session/CSRF integration, physical router, or hardware test was performed.
- **Remaining scope / next rotation:** this closes the current registered direct-EJ callback classification gap, not the entire HTTPD audit or repository governance programme. Continue rotating across the full userland: next prioritize firewall IPv6 restore/cleanup parity (or another highest-risk non-HTTPD module), then storage/mount/swap, network-service lifecycle, system administration/boot, authentication/upload/restore, and remaining services. ## Previous resumable checkpoint — Gate privileged direct ASP EJ actions

- **Main integration:** PR [#12](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/pull/12) squash-merged to `main` at `705876cf766e36d09bce695f53cf203016df05cc`. Base `main` SHA: `cf4ba992a4825c44e6ea2f50b963de6d5c2d5a25`; exact tested PR source head: `1bcdd24f6ef67004be9cc7ad2331c3612751929e`.

- **Finding:** `do_update_cgi()` already gated request-selected EJ outputs and direct storage ASP callbacks already had storage-specific authorization. But `ej.c::call_asp()` is a different dispatch path: it guarded storage mutators only. Other side-effect callbacks such as `asus_nvram_commit`, `notify_services`, `wan_action`, `wol_action`, `scutclient_action`, `mentohust_action`, `shadowsocks_action`, `adbyby_action`, and `update_action` could still be invoked from direct ASP wrappers by a logged-in session that did not satisfy the existing trusted-management-session predicate. Separately, `wol_action.asp` had an application-owned GET caller, bypassing the same-origin POST Origin/Referer gate.
- **Implemented:** added `httpd_direct_ej_policy.h`, composing the existing storage EJ policy with an explicit privileged callback set, and enforced it in `call_asp()` immediately before invoking a template handler. Converted the WOL caller in `Advanced_WOL_Content.asp` to `$j.post(..., 'json')`. Existing read-only direct callbacks retain their current behavior.

## Previous resumable checkpoint — Gate L2TP startup on confirmed transport exit

- **Main integration:** PR [#11](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/pull/11) squash-merged to `main` at `1c08dad0b115ec8744ac86272f929cae51c07dad`. Base `main` SHA: `0e44e545767a63522f8116b6348da4aacb2eb55c`; exact tested PR source head: `0d6a440c45aab1e87afd060d8bc50ef62925600d`.
- **Module:** WAN/PPP L2TP transport lifecycle, `trunk/user/rc/src/net_ppp.c::launch_wan_pppd()`.
- **Finding:** both L2TP backend branches called `kill_services()` but ignored its new integer result. Worse, the call happened only after `/tmp/ppp/options.wanN` had been opened and truncated and populated with credentials/options. If the old `l2tpd` or `xl2tpd` remained alive after SIGKILL, startup could still rewrite the shared options and proceed to launch/configure a replacement transport while the old daemon survived. This bypassed PR #10's fail-closed lifecycle result.
- **Implemented:** select the intended L2TP backend and stop its old daemon before opening/truncating the shared PPP options file. A failed stop logs the selected daemon, sets `errno=EBUSY`, and returns before any PPP/L2TP configuration publication. The selected backend is retained in `use_rpl2tp` for the later launch path; temporary `l2tp_wan_t` state is only set after the stop gate and completed PPP options-file write. The redundant unchecked kill calls were removed from the launch branches.
- **Changed paths:** `trunk/user/rc/src/net_ppp.c`; `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`.
- **Regression / exact-head CI:** the existing six-case GNU89 service-lifecycle policy regression remains in the HTTPD/security workflow; the production-source contract now asserts that the L2TP stop gate precedes PPP options-file open/truncation, that failure returns with `EBUSY`, and that both backend launch paths follow the successful gate. Exact PR-head Actions [run 42449814514](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42449814514) completed **SUCCESS** on exact source head `0d6a440c45aab1e87afd060d8bc50ef62925600d`; the host policy compile/run and bounded-service/WAN/L2TP source-contract step passed.
- **Remote persistence:** PR #11 merged by squash; `main` contains `1c08dad0b115ec8744ac86272f929cae51c07dad`. The exact-head PR Actions run is verified. A separate push-event Actions run for the squash SHA was not confirmed.
- **Validation boundary:** L1 source/control-flow review and L2 exact PR-head host CI PASS. No production MIPS/uClibc compile, complete firmware/TRX build, live L2TP/PPP session, target BusyBox process test, physical router or hardware validation was performed.
- **Residual / next:** WAN/PPP remains **AUDITING**, not closed. The callback path still has no per-session generation fence to prove stale `ip-up`/`ip-down` callbacks cannot race stop/reconnect; `udhcpc` SIGUSR2 and interface/static-route return propagation, watchdog/reconnect/backoff, target `pids()`/`killall` name semantics, other `kill_services()` callers, and IPv4 `LWF_LIST` vs IPv6 intent remain open. First inspect callback-vs-stop ordering and decide whether an explicit generation/ownership guard is needed; do not infer a fix without proving the callback lifecycle and compatibility constraints.
- **Repository-wide rotation:** HTTPD/HTTPS/CGI/auth/session/CSRF, firewall/IPv6, storage/mount/swap, system-management and other userland modules remain active in the global ledger. After the bounded callback-lifecycle check, rotate to the still-open HTTPD dynamic `update.cgi` EJ callback privilege/CSRF/session audit rather than continuing indefinitely in WAN or returning to routine SkipDBv2-only changes.

## Previous resumable checkpoint — Confirm service exit before WAN teardown

- **Main integration:** PR [#10](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/pull/10) merged by squash to main at bec69e232901e81ce937eb89ec1c05a0905293fc. Base main SHA: acfb164a4d0d5ec1b2109dfa0245e73508da1d1f; exact tested PR source head: b98f95574e0cee8b2f238dced474f7c54dae8c8d. Active branch for new work: main.
- **Module:** WAN/PPP service lifecycle and shared RC process-stop helper.
- **Finding:** common_ex.c::kill_services() returned void. After its SIGTERM wait expired, it sent SIGKILL, slept once, and never checked whether a target process remained. stop_wan_ppp() / stop_wan() therefore proceeded to clear NVRAM/interface state despite a surviving process. Full WAN restart and reconnect could then start a replacement session while an old PPP/DHCP worker remained active. Firmware flash similarly advanced to storage cleanup and flash after unchecked service termination.
- **Implemented:** added service_lifecycle_policy.h with callback-driven termination and a bounded final process-inventory check after SIGKILL (3 seconds). kill_services() now returns success only when all named processes are absent and logs survivors otherwise. WAN PPP/WAN stop functions propagate failure; manual disconnect, full restart and reconnect abort their next state transition on failure. stop_misc() now returns its result, and firmware update aborts before storage cleanup/flash if the PPP/wireless or miscellaneous stop groups remain active.
- **Regression:** added tests/service_lifecycle_policy.c covering graceful exit, forced exit, delayed exit after SIGKILL, a stubborn survivor, non-forced timeout, and an empty service list. The workflow compiles/runs it with GNU89 -Wall -Wextra -Werror and checks production caller ordering.
- **Exact-head CI:** PR Actions [run 42449267653](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42449267653) completed **SUCCESS** on exact tested source head b98f95574e0cee8b2f238dced474f7c54dae8c8d; the new bounded-service-termination step passed. The independent SkipDBv2 regression run [42449267646](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42449267646) also completed **SUCCESS** on the same PR head. No SkipDBv2 source was changed.
- **Changed paths:** .github/workflows/httpd-security.yml; trunk/user/rc/src/{common_ex.c,net_wan.c,rc.c,rc.h,services.c}; new service_lifecycle_policy.h and tests/service_lifecycle_policy.c.
- **Validation boundary:** L1 source/control-flow review and L2 exact PR-head CI PASS. These Actions runs validate host policy behavior and source contracts; they are **not** a production MIPS/uClibc build, complete firmware/TRX build, live PPP/router test, or hardware validation. No separate push-event run for the squash SHA is recorded here.
- **Residual:** this bounded change does not audit every kill_services() caller; shutdown remains best-effort by design. BusyBox pids()/killall process-name matching still needs target validation. The udhcpc SIGUSR2 request, interface/route command return values, stale PPP callback generation, automatic reconnect/watchdog/backoff, and IPv4 LWF_LIST versus IPv6 policy intent remain open.
- **Next exact action:** continue WAN/PPP with callback-versus-stop generation ordering and watchdog/reconnect/backoff, then inspect static-route and IPv6 interactions. Keep HTTPD/CGI/auth, firewall, storage and other userland modules on the repository-wide risk ledger; do not return to routine SkipDBv2-only work.

## Previous checkpoint — PPP callback identity validation (merged)

- **Main integration:** PR [#9](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/pull/9) merged to main at `3ad86ac7a295c2894570e47828a8a8fcc30a848b`. Re-fetch `main` before new code changes.

## Previous checkpoint — L2TP config-line validation and launcher error propagation (merged)

- **Main integration:** PR [#7](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/pull/7) merged to main at `ad3910f2ef0a88087cacff6f4fa2446f6dc52c11`. Re-fetch current `main` before new work.

- **Finding:** `safe_start_xl2tpd()` interpolated router hostname, WAN L2TP peer/username and VPN client peer/username from configuration into line-oriented `/etc/xl2tpd.conf` without CR/LF/control checks or bounds. Conditional `start_rpl2tp()` wrote a configured L2TP peer into `/etc/l2tp/l2tp.conf`, ignored some write/permission failures, ignored the `l2tpd` start result, and still requested a session.
- **Implemented:** shared `ppp_config_line_value_copy()` copies printable scalars into bounded destinations, rejecting C0/DEL controls and overflows with an empty output on failure. All active NVRAM-derived line values are checked before `xl2tpd.conf` is opened/truncated; config flush/close/chmod errors prevent daemon start. rp-l2tp validates the peer before opening its config, checks publication and permission results, stops on failed daemon launch, and propagates `l2tp-control start-session` status back to the caller. VPN-client status is only marked after successful config publication.
- **Regression:** the GNU89 `ppp_option_value_policy.c` host test now also covers single-line L2TP scalar values, CR/LF/tab rejection, exact buffer fit, overflow and empty output on failure. Workflow source contracts cover the xl2tpd and rp-l2tp callsites and enforce that daemon launch failure precedes session startup.
- **Changed files:** `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`, `trunk/user/rc/src/net_ppp.c`, `trunk/user/rc/src/ppp_option_value_policy.h`, `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/ppp_option_value_policy.c`.

- **Exact code/test/workflow SHA:** `5e90c2c6e3ecac982348457ead64cbd162dbf545`. Exact-head Actions [run 42447294943](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42447294943) completed **SUCCESS**; the workflow job finished successfully with all substantive steps green.
- **Finding:** `net_ppp.c::safe_pppd_line()` returned raw unescaped input when the output buffer could not fit the original, and silently truncated when escaping expanded beyond the buffer. Other quoted PPP values (`pptp_server`, `rp_pppoe_ac`, `rp_pppoe_service`) were written directly. `start_wan()` ignored `launch_wan_pppd()` failure, potentially leaving the provisional PPP interface as the recorded WAN/firewall interface.
- **Implemented:** new `ppp_option_value_policy.h` escapes quote/backslash characters, rejects control characters and fails on overflow; a separate mode permits LF/TAB only for the intentionally multiline custom `ppp_pppd` options. `net_ppp.c` uses checked writers for username, password, PPTP peer, PPPoE AC/service and custom options, aborts/cleans an incomplete file on invalid input, and records launch status. `net_wan.c` handles launch failure, restores effective `ifname_t` to the physical WAN interface, rebuilds firewall state, and skips demand wait. The workflow compiles/runs the new GNU89 policy test with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` and asserts all relevant source contracts.
- **Changed files:** `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`, `trunk/user/rc/src/net_ppp.c`, `trunk/user/rc/src/net_wan.c`, new `trunk/user/rc/src/ppp_option_value_policy.h`, new `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/ppp_option_value_policy.c`.
- **Validation boundary:** host-side policy compile/run plus exact-head Actions PASS. MIPS/uClibc build, full firmware/TRX generation, artifact inspection, real PPP connect/reconnect, live router and hardware tests are **NOT RUN**.
- **Remaining risk:** only generated PPP option-value syntax and launcher status/state recovery are closed here; WAN/PPP reconnect/watchdog lifecycle remains open. The multiline `ppp_pppd` custom option field remains supported and LF/TAB are deliberately allowed there, but CR and other controls are rejected. A more extensive lifecycle audit should examine failure/retry/backoff semantics and all transport-specific config writers. IPv4 `LWF_LIST` vs IPv6 filter parity and Adbyby extension rollback are still open from prior checkpoints.
- **Next exact action:** continue the WAN/PPP lifecycle audit across `net_wan.c`, `net_ppp.c`, reconnect/watchdog paths and generated config (especially L2TP config writers outside `launch_wan_pppd()`); inspect ignored service launcher return values and state transition reconciliation. Then rotate back to outstanding firewall IPv6 parity; do not resume routine SkipDBv2-only expansion.

## Previous checkpoint — Storage safe-eject failure propagation (merged)

- **Main integration:** PR [#5](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/pull/5) merged to main at `9d638965de658e4d2c40d26db2296f7c69e1c26d`. Current docs-only commits follow the merge; always fetch current `main` HEAD before editing code.
- **Exact tested code/test/workflow SHA:** `dd178c82ba05b916446d755193f0079fe76cad6c`. Exact-head GitHub Actions [run 42446872182](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42446872182) completed **SUCCESS** with 23 successful job steps.
- **Finding:** `services_stor.c::umount_dev()` ignored the return from `umount()`, attempted `rmdir()` regardless, and returned no status; `umount_stor_path()` ignored `swapoff()` / unmount failures and always requested drive spindown; `disk_initial.c::umount_all_storage()` also discarded global swapoff/unmount failures. This made a safe-eject path report completion and spin down while a filesystem or swap device might still be active.
- **Implemented:** `umount_dev()`, `umount_ejected()`, `umount_stor_path()`, and `umount_all_storage()` now propagate failure state. Mountpoints are only removed after successful unmount. Per-disk swapoff/unmount failures block `spindown_hdd()`; the requested-device filter no longer falls through when a disk identity is missing; the multi-port loop is retained. The safe-remove callers keep services/NFSD state conservative when cleanup is uncertain and record failure details.
- **Regression coverage:** the workflow's source/control-flow contract checks swapoff and unmount status propagation, ejected-device inventory/open/read failures, mountpoint cleanup ordering, spindown gating, caller orchestration and both push/PR path filters. Exact-head run passed including this new step.
- **Changed files:** `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`, `trunk/user/libdisk/src/disk_initial.c`, `trunk/user/libdisk/src/disk_initial.h`, `trunk/user/rc/src/services_stor.c`, and `trunk/user/rc/src/rc.h`.
- **Validation boundary:** source review and exact-head host CI PASS. No MIPS/uClibc target build, full firmware/TRX creation/artifact inspection, live mount/swap simulation, live router testing or hardware validation was run. CI here checks source/control-flow contracts, not a real kernel block-device lifecycle.
- **Known residuals:** `count_stor_mountpoint()` retains its legacy “count only” semantics (fopen failure still looks like zero) because existing start/restart callers treat nonzero as true; the safe-eject path additionally uses explicit unmount/ejected status to avoid treating uncertainty as a successful eject. Other service restart call sites should be audited separately. The full filesystem/mount topology race surface is not proven closed. IPv4 LAN/WAN `LWF_LIST` rules still have no corresponding obvious IPv6 path; intent must be established before altering network behavior. Adbyby add-on restore remains outside the core firewall rollback transaction.
- **Next exact task:** move to the WAN/PPP lifecycle module (not the VPN firewall interface guard already completed): inspect `trunk/user/rc/src/net_wan.c`, `net_ppp.c`, reconnect/watchdog paths and generated PPP configuration, map state transitions and error propagation, then add a bounded regression before changing behavior. Keep unresolved IPv6 filter parity in the ledger. Do not return to routine SkipDBv2-only expansion.

- **Implementation:** added `firewall_restore_transaction.h` with allowlisted family/table tokens, shell-safe private `/tmp` paths, once-per-table snapshots, sticky failure state and callback-driven rollback. `firewall_ex.c` now snapshots table state with `iptables-save`/`ip6tables-save` into `mkstemp` files restricted to mode 0600 before first mutation. If rollback itself fails, all recovery snapshots are retained and logged; IPv4 forwarding and, under `USE_IPV6`, IPv6 forwarding are disabled before the rebuild returns. Successful rollback leaves the previous rules in place and retains the existing forwarding setup behavior.
- **Regression / workflow:** added `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/firewall_restore_transaction.c` covering successful commit/one snapshot per table, reverse rollback after apply failure, snapshot failure, rollback-failure snapshot retention, explicit abort and invalid tokens. `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml` compiles/runs it with GNU89 warnings-as-errors and validates all ten core restore call sites, transaction wiring, extension-hook ordering and both IPv4/IPv6 forwarding fail-closed behavior.

## Previous resumable checkpoint — HTTPD authenticated POST Origin/Referer validation

- **Code/test/workflow commit on main:** `9110854745aced73ffb91318dc0e095099623d8e` (`security(httpd): reject cross-origin authenticated POSTs`). This checkpoint was built directly on main `9cd980aaa74f7eddca67d8f59001a31ac04ab851`; the current main code tree has been re-read from GitHub after the write. A follow-up governance-only commit updates this handoff, roadmap and development log; at the next start, fetch current main HEAD and verify it is a descendant of the code commit.
- **Actions:** [run 42437940928](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42437940928), workflow `HTTPD Authentication and Security Regression`, completed **SUCCESS** on the exact code SHA `9110854745aced73ffb91318dc0e095099623d8e`. The only job, `HTTPD authentication and disclosure policy`, passed all 21 steps, including `Compile and run policy regression` and `Verify HTTPD auth and route-access wiring`.
- **P1 finding addressed:** HTTPD previously did not capture or validate `Host`, `Origin`, or `Referer`. An authenticated browser's cross-origin POST could reach state-changing handlers (including apply/update/upload/restore routes and authenticated POST forms), and the auth path could create or refresh the shared IP-bound management login before route dispatch. Existing POST-only and per-callback policy gates do not themselves validate request origin.
- **Implementation:** `trunk/user/httpd/src/httpd.c` now copies Host/Origin/Referer into bounded request-local buffers, rejects duplicate or malformed metadata, and runs the same-origin check before the Basic-auth branch can establish or refresh the management session. New `httpd_csrf_policy.h` checks HTTP/HTTPS scheme, host (case-insensitive), effective numeric port, and bracketed IPv6 authority; an explicit Origin takes precedence, and a malformed/cross-origin Origin cannot be overridden by Referer. When Origin is absent, a present Referer is checked against the request Host. The policy drains the body and returns 403 for a denied authenticated POST. The request's existing TLS flag and legacy route/auth behavior are preserved.
- **Compatibility/security boundary:** when both Origin and Referer are absent, the policy intentionally allows the request to preserve legacy non-browser clients. Therefore this is a **best-effort browser CSRF mitigation, not complete CSRF protection**. A follow-up should assess synchronizer/request tokens and client/UI compatibility before tightening the no-metadata path. Also review reverse-proxy/Host deployment assumptions and URL authority corner cases.
- **Changed paths:** `trunk/user/httpd/src/httpd.c`; new `trunk/user/httpd/src/httpd_csrf_policy.h`; new `trunk/user/httpd/src/tests/httpd_csrf_policy.c`; `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`.
- **Validation:** focused host regression compiled and executed with `cc -std=gnu89 -Wall -Wextra -Werror`: PASS. Exact-head Actions run above: PASS (21/21 steps). Production MIPS/uClibc HTTPD object compilation, full firmware/TRX generation and artifact inspection, live HTTP integration, and physical router/hardware validation are **NOT RUN**.

## Previous completed checkpoint — Defer PID 1 asynchronous shutdown work to the main loop

- **Base main SHA before this task:** `8540f6aeb6b9e4582824052c0d630563e55f3678`. **Final code/test/workflow SHA:** `022cfdb6f906b936522ba05dae5fb74d93aa25e4` on `main`. Implementation was committed in two narrow, sequential main commits: `c59cfcc24b611eb95213c9ccd89d730d273dcfbf` (signal-state helper, event/shutdown handler split, regression and workflow) and `022cfdb6f906b936522ba05dae5fb74d93aa25e4` (close the signal-check/suspend lost-wakeup window).
- **Finding (P1/P2 lifecycle correctness):** PID 1's `catch_sig_fatal()` was registered for ordinary asynchronous termination signals (`SIGPIPE`, `SIGPWR`, `SIGQUIT`, `SIGTERM`) as well as synchronous CPU faults. It called stdio/string formatting, signal reconfiguration, service shutdown, process signalling, sleep/sync, system/reboot operations directly from signal context. `catch_sig_event()` also logged via stdio/string formatting, while the `SIGCHLD` reaper called `dprintf()` after `waitpid()`. `waitpid(WNOHANG)` is suitable in a signal handler; stdio logging is not.
- **Implementation:** separated synchronous faults (`SIGILL`, `SIGSEGV`, `SIGABRT`, `SIGFPE`, `SIGBUS`, `SIGSYS`, `SIGTRAP`) from ordinary shutdown requests. The `SIGPIPE`/`SIGPWR`/`SIGQUIT`/`SIGTERM` handler now only records the first request in `volatile sig_atomic_t` state and clears lower-priority queued work; the main PID 1 loop performs the existing graceful shutdown/reboot path outside signal context. Event handlers now only record `SIGUSR1`/`SIGALRM` flags. The SIGCHLD handler drains `waitpid(-1, NULL, WNOHANG)`, retries `EINTR`, and performs no logging. The wait loop blocks its queued-work signals while checking flags and uses `sigsuspend(original_mask)` to atomically unblock while sleeping; it restores/reapplies the mask around console-shell creation so an event cannot be lost between the check and sleep.
- **Changed paths:** `trunk/user/rc/src/init.c`; new `trunk/user/rc/src/pid1_signal_policy.h`; new `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/pid1_signal_policy.c`; `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`.
- **Regression/integration:** the GNU89 policy test covers event flags, shutdown priority/first-signal retention, clearing queued events on shutdown, and rejection of events after shutdown. The workflow runs it with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`, structurally checks that ordinary shutdown/event handlers contain no stdio or service calls, verifies the SIGCHLD waitpid/EINTR-only loop, checks signal routing and the atomic wait-mask pattern, and includes all changed paths in both push and pull-request filters.
- **Validation:** L1 scoped source review PASS. Local host regression `cc -std=gnu89 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` and execution PASS. Exact-head GitHub Actions run [42429745595](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42429745595) completed **SUCCESS** on final SHA `022cfdb6f906b936522ba05dae5fb74d93aa25e4`; all recorded job steps completed successfully, including the new PID 1 regression/source contracts and existing HTTPD, DHCP/DNS, PPP, firewall, storage and account checks. The immediately preceding implementation SHA `c59cfcc24b611eb95213c9ccd89d730d273dcfbf` also passed run [42429659005](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42429659005), but the final SHA's run is the authoritative checkpoint.
- **Explicit limits / residuals:** production MIPS/uClibc RC object build, full firmware/TRX build, and physical-router shutdown/reboot testing are **NOT RUN**. The synchronous CPU-fault handler still takes the legacy emergency shutdown path because returning would normally re-enter the faulting instruction; that path still invokes non-async-signal-safe operations and is not claimed fixed. Broader process ownership, PID-file trust, watchdog ownership, restart dependencies, shutdown ordering across every service, privilege transitions and signal disposition after exec remain AUDITING. `SIGUSR2` remains blocked/reserved and its previous notifier stub was a no-op; no new semantics are claimed.
- **Next repository-wide rotation:** continue with the P1 HTTPD/CGI/authentication surface rather than revisiting SkipDBv2 or repeating this PID 1 patch. Prioritize end-to-end `web_ex.c` CGI dispatch (`SystemCmd`, `sys_script()`, `action_script` update/apply routes), route authorization, command-buffer lifetime and privileged execution sinks; then retain the firewall rollback/IPv6, WAN/PPP and storage unmount/swap residuals in the global ledger.
- **Remote checkpoint branches:** the five existing `refactor/*` branches were compared against `main` before this task. Their ahead-only differences remain confined to SkipDBv2 code/build/tests/workflow/log (or are entirely behind); none was merged.

## Previous completed checkpoint — Secure the PID 1 privileged RC notification queue

- **Base main SHA before this task:** `fd534dcb77af90486d1bd1982fa68bd6d1e593ee`. **Code/test/workflow checkpoint SHA:** `57c52e27b4f2d1a2ab9ebbbd05af0247c2cbc305` on `main`. This task follows the explicit rotation from removable-storage repair into system init/service lifecycle; routine SkipDBv2-only work remains paused.
- **P1 finding — local privileged file truncation and action injection:** `dev_init.sh` created `/tmp/rc_notification` and `/tmp/rc_action_incomplete` as mode 0777 while PID 1 uses `umask(0000)`. Any local unprivileged process could plant queue entries for root PID 1 to dispatch (including reboot/service-control events). More seriously, `notify_rc.c` used path-based `fopen(..., "w")`, which followed an attacker-planted symlink from either world-writable directory and could truncate an arbitrary root-writable target when a privileged notification was emitted. This is a demonstrated trust-boundary flaw in the queue design; it does not, by itself, establish arbitrary code execution.
- **Fix:** both queue directories are now created as root-private mode 0700 immediately after `/tmp` is mounted and before `mdev -s` can trigger device notifications; explicit `chmod 700` pins the mode despite PID 1's zero umask. New `rc_notify_queue.h` centralizes strict ASCII event-name validation, root-owner/mode validation of directory descriptors, and regular-file marker checks (root owner, mode 0600, link count 1). Marker creation uses descriptor-relative `openat(O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW)`; queue consumers validate markers through the already-open private directory and remove them with `unlinkat`. `notify_rc.c` refuses to signal PID 1 if either queue directory or marker fails validation. `rc.c::handle_notifications()` now fails closed when the queue directories or marker do not meet the policy.
- **Regression and integration:** new `trunk/user/shared/src/tests/rc_notify_queue.c` covers valid/invalid names, 0700 directory ownership/mode, safe duplicate markers, 0600 marker mode, symlink victim preservation, wrong-mode markers, hardlinks, FIFOs and symlinked queue directories. The HTTPD/security workflow compiles/runs it with GNU89 `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`, parses `dev_init.sh`, checks production wiring and confirms all changed C paths are in both push and pull-request filters. The shared header is auto-installed by `shared/src/Makefile`'s existing `HEADERS` glob and consumed through the RC package's shared staging include path.
- **Persisted main commits:** helper `78cd856b7cc83e860777617be75ea2aca8541330`; regression `b6d2c10394f18ea9310f3a947d27600e5c8b07ed` (expanded wrong-mode coverage `0fab65f6c8fead7ddfd5b0ba80ba39b05f1a61ea)); production notification writer `2bf9f220f9fd0d5274025f70ce8b0f98d5b9bf5b` (diagnostic correction `a2986a4d11e99c8f65287b094d8a3b7026522afa)); PID 1 consumer `a66640cc5e6de2d76a26a788f1bf3a28c05c7a2f` (feature-test macro `58929dbe2867404dd0c113655d32f11274b31fad)); boot directory setup `ada795f3aa8ee36dcffc6e390edfdf8646bef1bc`; workflow wiring and source-contract follow-ups culminate at `57c52e27b4f2d1a2ab9ebbbd05af0247c2cbc305`.
- **Validation:** L1 scoped source review PASS. Host GNU89 regression PASS; AddressSanitizer/UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer regression PASS; isolated host compile of the changed `notify_rc.c` with queue header PASS; `dev_init.sh` syntax PASS under both host `sh -n` and BusyBox `ash -n`; source/path-filter/staging contracts PASS. The exact-head GitHub Actions push run is **NOT VERIFIED**: no completed run or success status was available at handoff time. Do not infer CI pass. Production RC/MIPS compile, full firmware/TRX build and physical-router tests are NOT RUN.

- **Still open:** this closes only the queue boundary, not the init/service audit. ## Previous resumable checkpoint — Block-storage whole-disk partition gate fails closed

- **Base main SHA:** `98bb2a1952f80b8c27658969a309a8457c263209`; **code/test/workflow SHA:** `3fbde30bbdf91a37380752b8e33148bd88e0d2a6`; current branch is `main`. This round resumes the explicitly unfinished block-storage hotplug audit after the completed USB modem metadata staging fix; no SkipDBv2-only branch work was merged.
- **Finding (P2 data-integrity / privileged hotplug path):** `hotplug_stor.c::check_root_partition()` returned `0` when `/proc/partitions` could not be opened. Both the SCSI and MMC whole-disk hotplug paths interpret zero as "no child partitions" and may then pass the raw disk device to `automount.sh` as a mountable partition. An unavailable inventory is not evidence that a disk is unpartitioned; the fallback could mount a whole partitioned block device and risks filesystem/data damage.
- **Change:** new `storage_partition_probe.h` validates SCSI disk names (`sd[a-z]+`) and MMC whole-disk names (`mmcblk[0-9]+`), detects only valid numeric child partitions (including MMC's `p` delimiter), and returns a tri-state result: partition present, no partition, or error. The production wrapper now returns an error on invalid names, failed `/proc/partitions` open, stream read error, and close error when no child was found. Existing whole-disk callers treat every nonzero result as a stop condition, so they fail closed instead of attempting the raw-disk mount.
- **Regression/integration:** new `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/storage_partition_probe.c` covers SCSI/MMC child detection, no-child cases, similarly prefixed non-partitions (`sdaa1`, `sdaX`), malformed device names and a missing input stream. `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml` compiles/runs it with GNU89 `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`, asserts production call-site wiring and includes the changed source/header/test in both push and pull-request path filters.
- **Changed paths:** `trunk/user/rc/src/hotplug_stor.c`; new `trunk/user/rc/src/storage_partition_probe.h`; new `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/storage_partition_probe.c`; `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`; this handoff, roadmap and dev log.
- **Validation:** L1 scoped source review PASS. Exact-head L2 Actions [run 42427177541](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42427177541) completed SUCCESS on `3fbde30bbdf91a37380752b8e33148bd88e0d2a6`; the job's 18 recorded steps all completed successfully, including GNU89 compile/run of the new regression and source-contract checks. Production RC/MIPS build, firmware/TRX build and physical-router validation are NOT RUN.
- **Still open:** this is one fail-closed partition-inventory correction, not a complete storage audit. Continue reviewing `automount.sh` mountpoint concurrency/ownership and filesystem-check gates; `services_stor.c::umount_dev()` ignores optware/umount results; swapoff and detach ordering, stale mountpoint cleanup, removable-media path provenance and libdisk enumeration remain open. Do not loosen filesystem permission defaults without target compatibility evidence.
- **Next action:** inspect the complete mount/unmount and swap lifecycle across `hotplug_stor.c`, `automount.sh`, `services_stor.c`, `libdisk/disk_initial.c` and `libdisk/dev_info.c`; add a focused regression for a confirmed defect before changing behavior. Then rotate to init/service lifecycle while keeping HTTPD/CGI/auth, firewall rollback/IPv6 and WAN/PPP on the whole-userland ledger.

No arbitrary include was added because that would allow persistent user configuration to override ACL and access boundaries without a compatibility contract.

## Previous resumable checkpoint — Storage account identity validation across libdisk, Unix passwd/group and Samba (exact-head Actions PASS)

- **Code/test/workflow SHA:** [`9702c96d355e712aad876b6371933974608bd96d`](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/commit/9702c96d355e712aad876b6371933974608bd96d) on `main`; exact-head GitHub Actions [run 42422590307](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42422590307) completed **SUCCESS**. All 16 job steps passed, including the new `Verify Samba and Unix account identity boundaries` step and the existing HTTPD, DNSMasq, DHCP, PPP, firewall and script-staging checks.
- **Finding:** the storage-account lifecycle had multiple identity consumers with non-identical policies. `libdisk::add_account()/mod_account()` allowed syntactically valid names that collide with system identities or the active web-management account; Samba ACL generation and `smbpasswd` provisioning used a separate token policy that did not consistently reject `root`, `nobody`, `sshd` or `anonymous`. Separately, `recreate_passwd_unix()` consumed raw NVRAM account slots while materializing `/etc/passwd` and `/etc/group`, without the common byte-level syntax check or duplicate suppression. This is a privileged identity-consistency and defense-in-depth finding; this review did **not** establish that an unauthenticated HTTP request can directly write arbitrary NVRAM or exploit the path.
- **Fix:** added `trunk/user/shared/src/include/router_account_policy.h`, a locale-independent ASCII account-name policy (1–31 bytes; alphanumeric, underscore, hyphen and dot; rejects `.` and `..`) plus a reserved-identity predicate for `root`, `nobody`, `sshd` and the active management username. `disk_share.c` now delegates its syntax rule to the shared helper and rejects reserved names on account creation/rename (also retaining the FTP `anonymous` restriction). `shutils.c::recreate_passwd_unix()` validates each raw NVRAM account before passwd/group output, excludes reserved and anonymous names, and suppresses duplicate account rows. `samba_share_policy.h::samba_acl_user_is_safe()` uses the common policy; `services_stor.c` adds a management-name-aware service guard before ACL emission and `smbpasswd` provisioning.
- **Regression and workflow:** added `trunk/user/shared/src/tests/router_account_policy.c` for valid/invalid byte cases, control/delimiter payloads, length boundaries and reserved identities; expanded `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/samba_share_policy.c` to reject `root`, `nobody`, `sshd` and `anonymous`. The workflow compiles both standalone policy tests with `cc -std=gnu89 -Wall -Wextra -Werror`, runs them, and checks the production source guard wiring. Both push and pull-request path filters now include the new libdisk source, shared header and shared test.
- **Changed paths:** `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`; `trunk/user/libdisk/src/disk_share.c`; `trunk/user/shared/src/shutils.c`; new `trunk/user/shared/src/include/router_account_policy.h`; new `trunk/user/shared/src/tests/router_account_policy.c`; `trunk/user/rc/src/samba_share_policy.h`; `trunk/user/rc/src/services_stor.c`; `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/samba_share_policy.c`.
- **Validation boundary:** L1 source review and L2 focused host policy regression/source-contract checks **PASS** at the exact tested SHA. Actions run 42422590307 passed all 16 steps. The workflow does not compile the complete production `libdisk`, `shutils` or RC/MIPS objects. Production MIPS build, firmware/TRX build and inspection, live Samba account/ACL tests, FTP tests and physical-router validation are **NOT RUN**. Existing reserved legacy account entries are not automatically migrated or deleted; rename/delete recovery and any existing Samba passdb state need a separate lifecycle review.
- **Remote checkpoint comparison against code/test/workflow SHA 9702c96d355e712aad876b6371933974608bd96d:** `refactor/bstream-tag-wire` 1 ahead / 583 behind; `refactor/dbapi-result-20261105` 8 / 664; `refactor/dynlib-lifecycle` 0 / 587; `refactor/sorting-boundaries` 0 / 583; `refactor/uarray-character-ctype` 2 / 581. All ahead-only deltas remain confined to SkipDBv2 code/build/tests/workflow/dev-log work; none was merged into this userland security task.
- **Next bounded task:** continue the FTP/Samba credential lifecycle: inspect password handling/argument exposure and `smbpasswd` status/error propagation, removal/rename of stale passdb identities, ACL semantics, and persistent `/etc/storage/smb.conf` precedence. Do not change writable-chroot/umask defaults without removable-filesystem compatibility evidence. Then rotate to USB/storage/hotplug and init/service lifecycle. Keep firewall cross-table rollback and IPv6 semantic parity open; do not pivot back to routine SkipDBv2-only work.

## Previous resumable checkpoint — Samba guest account no longer inherits UID 0 (exact-head Actions PASS)

- **Code/test/workflow checkpoint:** `966efa0380ffaa2ce18c702f0d2131e9e10b492d` on `main`; the preceding implementation commit is [`92bc47073d64f90c097ff7e8f2ce762eb878292b`](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/commit/92bc47073d64f90c097ff7e8f2ce762eb878292b). The matching corrected exact-head GitHub Actions [run 42421747255](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42421747255) completed **SUCCESS** on SHA `966efa0380ffaa2ce18c702f0d2131e9e10b492d`; all 15 job steps passed, including `Verify Samba share configuration trust boundaries`.
- **Finding:** `trunk/user/shared/src/shutils.c::recreate_passwd_unix(0)` writes the NVRAM `http_username` as UID/GID 0. In `trunk/user/rc/src/services_stor.c::write_smb_conf()`, Samba modes 1 and 3 previously emitted that same management username as `guest account` while setting `guest ok = yes` and `guest only = yes`. Thus anonymous SMB sessions could be mapped onto the router's root identity. This is a configuration-level privilege-boundary finding; no live router exploitation was attempted.
- **Fix:** introduced `SAMBA_GUEST_ACCOUNT "nobody"` in `samba_share_policy.h` and use it for generated guest-account configuration. The repository's account generator already creates `nobody` as UID/GID 99 with `/bin/false`, and its shadow entry is locked. Added regression assertions and workflow source contracts that ensure the guest branch uses this fixed account and does not reference `http_username`.
- **Changed paths:** `trunk/user/rc/src/services_stor.c`, `trunk/user/rc/src/samba_share_policy.h`, `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/samba_share_policy.c`, `.github/workflows/httpd-security.yml`. The new workflow checks both Samba and FTP policy binaries with `cc -std=gnu89 -Wall -Wextra -Werror`, checks the account database and source wiring, and retains the existing HTTPD/DNS/DHCP/PPP/firewall checks. Changed paths already appear in both push and pull-request filters.
- **CI correction trail:** earlier run [42421698737](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42421698737) failed at a Python source-contract assertion because the expected backslash spelling was over-escaped; both C policy regression binaries had already printed PASS. The assertion was corrected in commit `966efa0380ffaa2ce18c702f0d2131e9e10b492d`; its exact-head run 42421747255 is fully green.
- **Validation boundary:** L1 source inspection and focused L2 host policy regressions/source contracts **PASS** at the tested SHA. The workflow does not compile the complete production `services_stor.c` for MIPS. Production RC/MIPS build, firmware/TRX generation/inspection, live SMB client tests, removable-filesystem permission compatibility, and physical-router validation remain **NOT RUN**. Mapping guest access to `nobody` can change access to files whose ownership/modes relied on the former UID-0 mapping; validate compatible guest read/write behavior on target media before claiming runtime compatibility.
- **Remote checkpoint branches compared against tested SHA `966efa0380ffaa2ce18c702f0d2131e9e10b492d`:** `refactor/bstream-tag-wire` 1 ahead / 573 behind; `refactor/dbapi-result-20261105` 8 / 654; `refactor/dynlib-lifecycle` 0 / 577; `refactor/sorting-boundaries` 0 / 573; `refactor/uarray-character-ctype` 2 / 571. Ahead-only deltas remain confined to SkipDBv2, its tests/build/workflow and dev log; none was merged.

Then rotate to USB/storage/hotplug and init/service lifecycle. Keep firewall cross-table rollback and IPv6 semantic parity as separate open items; do not return to routine SkipDBv2-only work.

## Previous resumable checkpoint — FTP fail-closed mode and non-root worker configuration (exact-head Actions PASS)

- **Source/test/workflow SHA:** [`c258be09506ef66523924d3ed618bf6d5d3c7ae5`](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/commit/c258be09506ef66523924d3ed618bf6d5d3c7ae5), based on main `3b1174428facdb6b4c94e6a974832fdedbae15c5`. Exact-head GitHub Actions [run 42420569075](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42420569075) completed **SUCCESS** on that SHA. This adds the FTP policy helper/test and workflow source contracts; all job steps, including the `Verify Samba share configuration trust boundaries` step, completed successfully.
- **Finding 1 — invalid FTP mode fails open:** `Advanced_AiDisk_others.asp` exposes only modes 1–4, but `write_vsftpd_conf()` used the raw `st_ftp_mode` integer. Anything other than exactly mode 2 followed a branch with `anonymous_enable=YES`, so malformed or stale values could enable anonymous access. `ftp_share_policy.h::ftp_share_mode_normalize()` preserves all four supported modes and maps every other value to mode 2 (local login enabled, anonymous login disabled).
- **Finding 2 — FTP worker privilege drop:** generated `vsftpd.conf` forced `nopriv_user=root`. It now selects `nobody`, which the firmware creates as UID/GID 99 with `/bin/false` and a locked shadow entry; bundled vsftpd also defaults to `nobody`. This removes the explicit root worker identity, but the bundled manual recommends a dedicated service account rather than sharing `nobody`; that remains a follow-up rather than being claimed solved.
- **Compatibility decision still open:** `allow_writable_root=YES` was deliberately left unchanged. Inspection of `trunk/user/vsftpd/src/secutil.c` shows the tunable bypasses the refusal to chroot when the chroot root is writable by the unprivileged user. Turning it off without checking the permissions and semantics of each removable-media filesystem may break FTP access. Anonymous-upload modes and `anon_umask=000` / `local_umask=000` also need a permissions-compatibility review. `allow_writeable_chroot` is a distinct option and is not being conflated with this custom tunable.
- **Regression coverage:** new `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/ftp_share_policy.c` tests that modes 1–4 are preserved and 0, negative, out-of-range, `INT_MIN` and `INT_MAX` normalize to mode 2. The workflow compiles it with `cc -std=gnu89 -Wall -Wextra -Werror`, runs it, checks config wiring / privilege identity and confirms push/PR path lists remain identical (59 entries each). A standalone local GNU89 `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` harness also passed.
- **Validation boundary / remaining scope:** source and focused host regression plus exact-head Actions PASS; production RC/MIPS build, firmware/TRX validation, FTP runtime and physical-router testing were **NOT RUN**. FTP/Samba remains **AUDITING**. Samba ACL/account-password lifecycle, custom `/etc/storage/smb.conf`, FTP dedicated service identity, writable chroot / umask compatibility, NFS `no_root_squash`, USB/storage/hotplug and full service lifecycle remain open.
- **Remote checkpoint comparison against tested SHA `c258be09506ef66523924d3ed618bf6d5d3c7ae5`:** `refactor/bstream-tag-wire` 1 ahead / 571 behind; `refactor/dbapi-result-20261105` 8 / 652; `refactor/dynlib-lifecycle` 0 / 575; `refactor/sorting-boundaries` 0 / 571; `refactor/uarray-character-ctype` 2 / 569. Ahead-only deltas remain in SkipDBv2/CMake/test/workflow/dev-log files; none was merged.
- **Next bounded task:** finish FTP/Samba account lifecycle, anonymous write/chroot/umask compatibility and the dedicated service identity decision; then rotate to USB/storage/hotplug and init/service lifecycle. Keep firewall restore rollback and IPv6 semantic parity as distinct open tasks. Do not return to routine SkipDBv2-only work.

## Previous resumable checkpoint — FTP/Samba generated-configuration token validation (exact-head Actions PASS)

- **Implementation and verification SHAs:** production guards, policy helper and test were committed in [`d46cc92f61dcda300307d3a2eb0b119a41a1d7b8`](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/commit/d46cc92f61dcda300307d3a2eb0b119a41a1d7b8); the workflow source-contract correction is [`309fa56ce881c1c3b1bea755c057acdbb73b3fa9`](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/commit/309fa56ce881c1c3b1bea755c057acdbb73b3fa9). Exact-head Actions run [`42419984033`](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42419984033) completed **SUCCESS** at `309fa56ce881c1c3b1bea755c057acdbb73b3fa9`; all 15 job steps passed. The first run [42419930875](https://github.com/xiaobei206/padavan-4.4-2/actions/runs/42419930875) compiled and ran the new C policy test successfully, then failed only because its Python source contract looked for a block-comment spelling while the existing source used a `//` comment. The follow-up corrected the assertion to the actual source marker; run `42419984033` is the verified exact-head result.
- **Finding / scope:** `trunk/user/rc/src/services_stor.c::write_smb_conf()` interpolated filesystem-derived mount points, disk labels, partition/folder names and account-list values into Samba section headers and directive lines without a common token policy. Control characters/newlines can create additional configuration lines; section delimiters and comma/whitespace-bearing ACL tokens can alter the intended share/authorization configuration. This is a configuration-output integrity finding, not proof of a remotely reachable code-execution path.
- **Fix:** new `samba_share_policy.h` rejects control bytes, DEL, backslash line-continuation characters and percent substitution in configuration text; constrains section names to nonempty <=255-byte path components without slash, section delimiters, comment delimiters, or `.`/`..`; and requires generated storage paths to stay under `/media/` with no empty/dot/dot-dot components. Samba partition and per-folder shares are skipped on unsafe input, using fixed log messages that do not echo the raw value. Samba usernames are constrained before output to all four ACL lists, before use as the guest-account token, and before being passed as the username argument to `smbpasswd`. The FTP banner now validates `productid` before emitting it to `vsftpd.conf`, with a fixed fallback for unsafe values.
- **Regression / CI:** `trunk/user/rc/src/tests/samba_share_policy.c` covers ordinary/UTF-8 share names, empty/null inputs, CR/LF/control payloads, backslash and percent expansion, section delimiters, slash/dot-dot traversal, overlong names, mount paths outside `/media/`, malformed path components and unsafe ACL usernames/option-like names. Exact-head run `42419984033` compiled and ran the GNU89 `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` test, then passed the production source-wiring assertions and all previous HTTPD/DNS/DHCP/firewall/script regression steps. The push and pull-request path filters both include the new source/header/test paths (57 entries each; same set).
- **Validation boundary / open items:** L1 source review and focused L2 host-policy/source-contract validation PASS. The production RC MIPS object, target firmware/TRX build, Samba/vsftpd runtime, and physical-router testing were **NOT RUN**. FTP/Samba remains **AUDITING**, not module-closed. Anonymous FTP modes, writable chroot and `nopriv_user=root`, Samba share permissions and account/password lifecycle, user-maintained `/etc/storage/smb.conf`, NFS `no_root_squash`, alternate consumers and service restart/error handling remain open.
- **Remote checkpoint branches compared against tested SHA `309fa56ce881c1c3b1bea755c057acdbb73b3fa9`:** `refactor/bstream-tag-wire` 1 ahead / 569 behind; `refactor/dbapi-result-20261105` 8 / 650; `refactor/dynlib-lifecycle` 0 / 573; `refactor/sorting-boundaries` 0 / 569; `refactor/uarray-character-ctype` 2 / 567. Every ahead-only delta is limited to SkipDBv2 source/CMake/tests/workflow/dev log; none was merged.
- **Next bounded task:** continue the FTP/Samba credential, anonymous-mode, write-permission and privilege-drop review (especially `nopriv_user=root` and `allow_writable_root=YES`, with target compatibility verified before changing defaults). Then proceed to USB/storage/hotplug and init/service lifecycle. Keep firewall restore rollback and IPv6 semantic parity as separate open tasks; do not return to routine SkipDBv2-only expansion.
